What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because... I keep getting these flashes, you know? Just out of nowhere. Like, I'll be standing at a pickup counter waiting for a package—I'm a courier, so I'm always at these counters. Some of them are really nice, they offer you water or coffee, but most of them just ignore you until the package is ready. The worst is when they make you wait outside in the rain. But anyway, I'll just be standing there, and suddenly it's like I'm right back in that crowded holding room. I can see the fight breaking out, I can hear everybody getting shoved against the wall. It's so loud in my head. And it's weird because the last time I was at a counter, the guy behind it had this really loud ringtone, sounded like an old rotary phone, and it almost made me jump out of my skin. But yeah, those flashes, they just keep hitting me when I least expect it.\n\nDoctor: What do you think triggers these flashes?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't even know. I've tried to figure it out, like, is it loud noises? But sometimes it's perfectly quiet when it happens. Like yesterday, I was driving my route in one of the new transit vans—my company just leased these new electric ones, and they are super quiet. Almost too quiet, you know? You don't even hear the engine turn on. I actually almost backed into a loading dock last week because I couldn't hear the hum, and the backup camera was totally smudged with mud. You'd think they'd wash the fleet more often, especially since we're supposed to be representing the brand, but they're cutting costs everywhere right now. They even stopped giving us the good gel pens for signatures. We just get these cheap plastic ones that leak in your pocket. It's ridiculous. \n\nBut... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, triggers. Yeah, no, I haven't noticed any kind of pattern at all. I really have no idea.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain places or situations because of these flashes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I do. I've actually been turning down all the night runs lately. Which kind of sucks because the night dispatch guy, Gary, is actually pretty cool. He lets us pick our own radio stations in the warehouse while we load up, unlike the morning shift where it's just top 40 pop music on a loop. I swear if I hear that same pop song again I'm gonna lose my mind. But yeah, I've been taking the long way around town on my routes, too. \n\nThe GPS they make us use is supposed to optimize for time, but it keeps trying to route me the fastest way, and I just... I can't make myself go near that detention building. I just can't do it. And I really try to avoid talking about what happened, too, because even right now, just bringing it up makes me feel like I'm right back there in that room. So I just drive the long way. It adds like twenty minutes to my route, and there's always construction on the bypass right now. They've been fixing that same stretch of road for three months. Have you noticed that? The city just leaves those orange cones out forever and nobody is ever actually working on the road. It totally wrecks my delivery metrics.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific sounds that you try to avoid because they might trigger these flashes?\n\nPatient: Sounds? Um... no, not that I can think of. I haven't really noticed any specific noises I try to stay away from. Like I said before, sometimes it happens when it's completely quiet anyway. \n\nI do try to keep something playing in the van just to have some background noise, though. I've actually been getting really into podcasts lately while I'm doing my deliveries. I used to listen to all those true crime ones, but honestly, they get so repetitive after a while. It's always the husband or the business partner, you know? So now I'm listening to this massive history series about ancient Rome. It is crazy interesting. Did you know they had, like, actual fast food places back then? They had these little street counters where regular people could just walk up and buy hot bread and cheese and stuff. \n\nI was actually just telling my buddy Dave about it—he drives the downtown grid. Man, I do not envy him at all. Parking downtown is an absolute nightmare right now. They just raised the meter rates to like four bucks an hour, and the city parking enforcement guys are just ruthless. I swear they hide in the alleys and just wait for your meter to blink red. And our company won't even reimburse us for the tickets anymore! It comes straight out of our paychecks now, which is... I mean, how is that even legal? We're doing our jobs! \n\nBut, uh... wait, sorry. Sounds. Right, you were asking about sounds. Yeah, no, I don't think there are any specific sounds I avoid. I haven't noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding people or social situations because of these flashes?\n\nPatient: Avoiding people? Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I still hang out with my buddies and stuff. \n\nActually, a few of us from the depot went to grab a beer just this past Friday at that sports bar over on 4th Street. Have you been there since they remodeled? They totally ripped out all the old wooden booths and put in this weird, like, industrial metal seating. It is so uncomfortable. You sit there for twenty minutes and your lower back starts absolutely killing you. Plus, they got rid of the half-price appetizer menu during happy hour, which was literally the only reason we ever went there. Now a plate of nachos is like sixteen bucks, and they barely put any cheese on it. It's just a mountain of dry tortilla chips with a little bit of salsa scooped on top. It's a total rip-off. \n\nMy buddy Marcus actually complained to the waitress about it, and she gave him this massive attitude. Which, I get it, she doesn't make the prices, but still, you don't have to roll your eyes, right? Customer service is just completely dead these days. Nobody cares anymore. \n\nBut, uh... wait. Did you ask about avoiding people? Yeah, no, I'm not really doing that. I still go out and see my friends and everything. I haven't noticed the flashes stopping me from being around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being easily startled?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I really do. It's like I'm constantly on edge. Every time the radio in the van crackles, or I hear a siren, or someone just slams a door too hard, I jump like I'm about to get rushed. My heart starts pounding so hard afterward that I literally have to pull over or step outside just to catch my breath. \n\nIt happened just yesterday, actually. I was pulled over near that new coffee shop on Elm—the one with the ridiculous line that goes all the way out the door and around the block. I don't get the hype, honestly. They charge like seven dollars for a regular iced coffee, and they don't even use normal ice cubes. They use those weird, giant spherical ice cubes that take up the entire cup, so you're really only getting like three sips of actual coffee. And the lids never stay on right. I spilled half a cup on my passenger seat last week, and now the whole van smells like burnt caramel and stale milk. I tried using that upholstery cleaner stuff you get at the auto parts store, but it just made it smell like burnt caramel and harsh chemicals. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, someone dropped a metal tray or something outside the shop, and I practically jumped out of my skin. My chest was tight for like twenty minutes after that. I just had to sit there and wait for my heart to stop hammering before I could even put the van back in drive. It's exhausting, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're constantly on the lookout for potential dangers?\n\nPatient: On the lookout? Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I'm just driving my route, trying to get through the day. I don't feel like I'm scanning for danger or anything like that. \n\nAlthough, speaking of looking out for things, you really have to watch out for the potholes over on the west side right now. It is unbelievable. The city was supposed to repave that whole stretch of Washington Avenue last summer, but then they just... didn't. And now with the rain we've been getting, there are these craters that could literally swallow a tire. I hit one last week near the old textile factory, and I swear I thought my suspension was gone. The van made this horrible crunching sound. \n\nI actually had to take it into the shop, and the mechanic—this guy Tony, I've been going to him for years—he told me he's seen like ten cars this week with busted tie rods just from that one street. And he's charging a fortune for labor now. He said it's because of inflation and supply chain issues with parts, but I mean, come on. A hundred and fifty bucks an hour just for labor? It's getting to the point where it's cheaper to just buy a new vehicle than fix the one you have. Which is crazy, because the used car market is completely wrecked right now anyway. My brother tried to buy a used Honda Civic last month, and they wanted almost twenty grand for a car with eighty thousand miles on it. For a Civic! \n\nBut, uh... what were we talking about? Oh, right, looking out for danger. Yeah, no, I don't really do that. I'm just trying to avoid the potholes and get my deliveries done, you know? I haven't noticed myself constantly looking out for threats or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're waiting for something bad to happen?\n\nPatient: Waiting for something bad to happen? Um... no, not really. I mean, I don't sit around expecting the sky to fall or anything. I just go about my day, you know?\n\nThough, I will say, waiting for things in general is getting ridiculous lately. Have you tried calling customer service for anything recently? I had to call my internet provider on Tuesday because the Wi-Fi at my apartment kept dropping. Like, every twenty minutes, it would just cut out. So I call them up, and I swear I was on hold for an hour and forty-five minutes. And they play that awful, repetitive elevator music that loops every thirty seconds. It gets stuck in your head for the rest of the day. \n\nWhen I finally got a real person, it was this guy who clearly had no idea what he was doing. He just kept reading from a script, telling me to unplug the router and plug it back in. Like, obviously I already tried that! I'm not an idiot. Then he tried to tell me I needed to upgrade to their premium package for an extra thirty bucks a month if I wanted a \"more stable connection.\" It's such a scam. They throttle your speed on purpose just so they can upsell you. My neighbor switched to that fiber optic company that just moved into town, and he says it's amazing, but they have to drill a hole in your wall to install it, and my landlord is super strict about that kind of stuff. He threw a fit last year just because I used pushpins to hang up a calendar. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? Oh, right, waiting for bad stuff. No, I don't really do that. I'm not waiting for disaster or anything. I just hate waiting on hold.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Detached? Like, from my body or something? Uh, no. I don't think I've ever felt like that. I'm pretty much just right here, you know? Paying attention to my surroundings. \n\nActually, speaking of surroundings, the scenery over by the new development on the east side is completely unrecognizable now. Have you been over there recently? They tore down that entire strip of woods where the old drive-in theater used to be. It's just completely gone. Now it's all these cookie-cutter townhouses that look exactly the same. They're painted these weird shades of gray and beige. I had a delivery there on Tuesday, and I swear I drove around in circles for like fifteen minutes because none of the streets have signs up yet. And the GPS hasn't updated, so my phone just keeps telling me I'm driving through an empty field. \n\nIt drains my battery like crazy when the GPS gets confused like that. I actually had to buy one of those portable power banks, but I couldn't even use it yesterday because my cat knocked the charging cable off the counter and hid it. He does that with everything. Last week he batted my van keys under the fridge and I was late for my shift. My supervisor, Greg, gave me this whole lecture about punctuality, which is incredibly rich coming from a guy who takes two-hour lunch breaks to go to the driving range. I tried to point that out, but he just threatened to put me on the graveyard shift. \n\nBut, wait, what was the question? Detached? Yeah, no, I don't feel detached from myself or anything. I'm just trying to figure out where I'm going half the time without my phone dying.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Trouble sleeping? Yeah, actually. I barely sleep now. I'll maybe doze off for an hour or two, but then I'm up from some dream about that cell, or I'll hear a door slamming and just jolt awake. And by morning, I feel way too wrecked to drive my route. \n\nWhich is a huge problem right now because they just completely changed the delivery zones for the downtown area, and it is a total disaster. My supervisor, Greg—the one I was telling you about—decided we needed to \"optimize\" our routes using this new software the corporate office bought. But the software doesn't account for one-way streets or the construction on 5th Avenue. So yesterday, it routed me right into this narrow alley that was completely blocked off by a garbage truck. Those sanitation guys take forever, too. I was sitting there for twenty minutes watching this guy slowly empty dumpsters while he was casually talking on his phone. And you can't even back out of that alley because people park right up against the curb. I had to just sit there listening to the radio, and every single station was playing commercials. Have you noticed how many commercials there are for sports betting apps now? It's like every other ad is some actor telling you to bet on football. It's getting out of hand. My buddy Dave lost like three hundred bucks last weekend betting on a game because he thought he had a \"sure thing.\" I told him the house always wins, but he never listens to me. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what was the question? Sleep? Right, yeah. I barely sleep. Just waking up from those dreams and feeling too wrecked to drive.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you are on the lookout for potential threats or dangers in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Threats or dangers? No, not really. I mean, I'm not walking around looking over my shoulder or expecting someone to jump out at me. I'm just trying to get through the day, you know? \n\nSpeaking of getting through the day, though, you wouldn't believe the hazard it is just trying to get a decent cup of coffee around here anymore. That little diner I usually stop at on 3rd Street? They completely changed their menu, and now everything is, like, artisanal and overpriced. I just wanted a regular black coffee, and the barista—who was wearing this ridiculous leather apron, by the way—started asking me if I wanted a pour-over or a cold brew or some single-origin roast from Guatemala. Like, buddy, I'm in a delivery van, I just need caffeine. And then he charged me six dollars! Six dollars for a plain coffee! \n\nAnd the cups they use now are those compostable ones, which sounds great for the environment, I guess, but the lids don't stay on. I hit one pothole on Elm Street—and the potholes on Elm are basically craters right now because the city hasn't fixed them since winter—and the lid pops off, splashing hot coffee all over my dashboard. I had to use half a roll of paper towels to clean it up, and it still smells like burnt beans in my van. Plus, it got all over the manifest clipboard. Greg, my supervisor, is going to throw an absolute fit if he sees coffee stains on the paperwork. He treats those manifests like they're sacred documents or something, even though everything is supposed to be digital now anyway. \n\nBut, uh... what were we talking about? Oh, right, looking out for danger. No, I don't really do that. I'm not on the lookout for threats or anything. Just trying to avoid potholes and overpriced coffee.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're constantly watching for something to go wrong?\n\nPatient: Watching for something to go wrong? I mean, no, not really. I'm not, like, anticipating disaster at every turn or anything. I just kind of do my job. \n\nThough, honestly, speaking of things going wrong, you should see the new sorting machine they installed at the depot. It is an absolute nightmare. They spent I don't even know how many thousands of dollars on this automated conveyor system that's supposed to scan barcodes and route packages to the right loading bays. But the scanner gets jammed if a label is even slightly wrinkled. And since it's an automated system, when one box jams, the whole line stops. Every single morning this week, we've had to stand around for like forty-five minutes while some tech guy in a high-vis vest climbs up there with a flashlight to unjam it. \n\nAnd the worst part is, while we're standing there, the warehouse gets freezing cold. They keep the loading dock doors open, and the heating system in that building is basically nonexistent. I brought it up to Greg—my supervisor—and he told me to \"layer up.\" Layer up! I'm already wearing a thermal shirt and a hoodie under my uniform jacket. I look like a marshmallow. Plus, you can't even move properly to carry the heavier boxes when you're wearing that much clothing. I nearly dropped a mini-fridge on my foot on Wednesday because my sleeves were so bulky I couldn't get a good grip on the straps. If I broke my foot, I don't even know how workers' comp would handle it, because Greg still hasn't filed the paperwork from when Dave twisted his ankle on the loading ramp last month. Dave's been limping around the depot complaining about it to anyone who will listen. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Watching for something to go wrong? Oh, right. No, I don't really do that. I just deal with things when they happen, you know? Like the sorting machine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible for what happened in that holding room?\n\nPatient: Guilty? No, why would I feel guilty? I didn't do anything wrong in there. I didn't cause it. I was just... there. \n\nSpeaking of being there, though, you know who wasn't there when I got back to the depot yesterday? Greg. He just vanished around 2:00 PM. Left his office door wide open, computer still on, half-eaten sandwich on his desk. Tuna salad, I think, because the whole hallway smelled like it. Which is so hypocritical, by the way, because last week he sent out this mass email to all the drivers saying we weren't allowed to eat strong-smelling foods in the breakroom anymore. Someone microwaved leftover salmon, and Greg threw an absolute fit. He printed out these laminated signs and taped them to the microwaves. \"Be considerate of your coworkers' olfactory space.\" Olfactory space! Who even talks like that? \n\nAnyway, so Greg is just gone, right? And I had this package that needed a supervisor's override because the barcode got torn off. The customer was this older lady who called the depot three times asking where her grandson's birthday present was. But Dave thinks he just left early to go play golf, because Dave saw his clubs in the backseat of his car on Tuesday. Dave is always snooping around people's cars in the lot. Just load your van. \n\nBut, uh... what were we talking about? Guilt? Right, no. I don't feel responsible for what happened. I didn't have anything to do with it. I got a ticket last Thursday for being two minutes over my meter. Two minutes! I was literally walking out of the lobby of the office building. I waved at the guy, like, hey, I'm right here, I'm leaving! And he just printed the little slip and stuck it under my wiper without even making eye contact with me. Sixty-five bucks! \n\nAnd the worst part is, the company doesn't reimburse us for parking tickets unless we can prove the loading zone was illegally blocked by a civilian vehicle. I had to park a block away and haul three boxes of printer paper down the sidewalk. I honked at him when I drove past later, and he actually flipped me off! ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Avoid thinking about it? I mean, I guess I have been taking the long way around town lately. I can't even make myself go near that detention building anymore. I've even been turning down night runs just to make sure I don't get routed near there. If I even try to talk about it or think about what happened, it feels like I'm right back in that room.

Which, by the way, turning down night runs is really messing with my paycheck. The night premium is an extra two dollars an hour, and that adds up, you know? Especially right now, because my landlord just raised my rent. He sent this letter saying it's due to "increased property taxes and maintenance costs," but he hasn't fixed a single thing in that apartment since I moved in. The gasket on the refrigerator door has been peeling off for six months. I have to push a kitchen chair against it to keep it shut tight, otherwise the cold air leaks out and all my milk goes bad. I buy the good milk, too—the lactose-free kind, because regular milk upsets my stomach. It's like five dollars a carton. 

And don't even get me started on the laundry situation in my building. There are three washers for twenty units, and one of them has had an "Out of Order" sign taped to it since Thanksgiving. Thanksgiving! It's ridiculous. People just leave their wet clothes sitting in the other two machines for hours. I actually had to take someone's damp towels out and put them on top of the dryer on Sunday because I'd been waiting for an hour and a half to wash my uniforms. If I don't wash my uniforms, Greg writes me up. 

Wait, what did you ask me? Avoiding talking about it? Yeah, I guess I do. I just... I can't go near that building or talk about it without feeling like I'm right back there. It's too much.

Some of them are really nice, they offer you water or coffee, but most of them just ignore you until the package is ready. The worst is when they make you wait outside in the rain. But anyway, I'll just be standing there, and suddenly it's like I'm right back in that crowded holding room. I can see the fight breaking out, I can hear everybody getting shoved against the wall. It's so loud in my head. You don't even hear the engine turn on. I actually almost backed into a loading dock last week because I couldn't hear the hum, and the backup camera was totally smudged with mud. You'd think they'd wash the fleet more often, especially since we're supposed to be representing the brand, but they're cutting costs everywhere right now. They even stopped giving us the good gel pens for signatures. We just get these cheap plastic ones that leak in your pocket. It's ridiculous. \n\nBut... wait, what did you ask me? I swear if I hear that same pop song again I'm gonna lose my mind. I can't make myself go near that detention building. I just can't do it. It adds like twenty minutes to my route, and there's always construction on the bypass right now. They've been fixing that same stretch of road for three months. Have you noticed that? The city just leaves those orange cones out forever and nobody is ever actually working on the road. It totally wrecks my delivery metrics.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific sounds that you try to avoid because they might trigger these flashes?\n\nPatient: Sounds? Um... no, not that I can think of. I haven't really noticed any specific noises I try to stay away from. Like I said before, sometimes it happens when it's completely quiet anyway. \n\nI do try to keep something playing in the van just to have some background noise, though. I've actually been getting really into podcasts lately while I'm doing my deliveries. I used to listen to all those true crime ones, but honestly, they get so repetitive after a while. It's always the husband or the business partner, you know? So now I'm listening to this massive history series about ancient Rome. It is crazy interesting. Did you know they had, like, actual fast food places back then? They had these little street counters where regular people could just walk up and buy hot bread and cheese and stuff. \n\nI was actually just telling my buddy Dave about it—he drives the downtown grid. Man, I do not envy him at all. Parking downtown is an absolute nightmare right now. They just raised the meter rates to like four bucks an hour, and the city parking enforcement guys are just ruthless. I swear they hide in the alleys and just wait for your meter to blink red. And our company won't even reimburse us for the tickets anymore! It comes straight out of our paychecks now, which is... I mean, how is that even legal? We're doing our jobs! \n\nBut, uh... wait, sorry. Sounds. Right, you were asking about sounds. Yeah, no, I don't think there are any specific sounds I avoid. I haven't noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding people or social situations because of these flashes?\n\nPatient: Avoiding people? Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I still hang out with my buddies and stuff. \n\nActually, a few of us from the depot went to grab a beer just this past Friday at that sports bar over on 4th Street. Have you been there since they remodeled? They totally ripped out all the old wooden booths and put in this weird, like, industrial metal seating. It is so uncomfortable. You sit there for twenty minutes and your lower back starts absolutely killing you. Plus, they got rid of the half-price appetizer menu during happy hour, which was literally the only reason we ever went there. Now a plate of nachos is like sixteen bucks, and they barely put any cheese on it. It's just a mountain of dry tortilla chips with a little bit of salsa scooped on top. It's a total rip-off. \n\nMy buddy Marcus actually complained to the waitress about it, and she gave him this massive attitude. Which, I get it, she doesn't make the prices, but still, you don't have to roll your eyes, right? Customer service is just completely dead these days. Nobody cares anymore. \n\nBut, uh... wait. Did you ask about avoiding people? Yeah, no, I'm not really doing that. I still go out and see my friends and everything. I haven't noticed the flashes stopping me from being around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being easily startled?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I really do. It's like I'm constantly on edge. Every time the radio in the van crackles, or I hear a siren, or someone just slams a door too hard, I jump like I'm about to get rushed. My heart starts pounding so hard afterward that I literally have to pull over or step outside just to catch my breath. \n\nIt happened just yesterday, actually. I was pulled over near that new coffee shop on Elm—the one with the ridiculous line that goes all the way out the door and around the block. I don't get the hype, honestly. They charge like seven dollars for a regular iced coffee, and they don't even use normal ice cubes. They use those weird, giant spherical ice cubes that take up the entire cup, so you're really only getting like three sips of actual coffee. And the lids never stay on right. My chest was tight for like twenty minutes after that. I just had to sit there and wait for my heart to stop hammering before I could even put the van back in drive. It's exhausting, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're constantly on the lookout for potential dangers?\n\nPatient: On the lookout? Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I'm just driving my route, trying to get through the day. I don't feel like I'm scanning for danger or anything like that. \n\nAlthough, speaking of looking out for things, you really have to watch out for the potholes over on the west side right now. It is unbelievable. The city was supposed to repave that whole stretch of Washington Avenue last summer, but then they just... didn't. And now with the rain we've been getting, there are these craters that could literally swallow a tire. I hit one last week near the old textile factory, and I swear I thought my suspension was gone. The van made this horrible crunching sound. \n\nI actually had to take it into the shop, and the mechanic—this guy Tony, I've been going to him for years—he told me he's seen like ten cars this week with busted tie rods just from that one street. And he's charging a fortune for labor now. He said it's because of inflation and supply chain issues with parts, but I mean, come on. A hundred and fifty bucks an hour just for labor? It's getting to the point where it's cheaper to just buy a new vehicle than fix the one you have. Which is crazy, because the used car market is completely wrecked right now anyway. My brother tried to buy a used Honda Civic last month, and they wanted almost twenty grand for a car with eighty thousand miles on it. For a Civic! \n\nBut, uh... what were we talking about? Oh, right, looking out for danger. Yeah, no, I don't really do that. I'm just trying to avoid the potholes and get my deliveries done, you know? I haven't noticed myself constantly looking out for threats or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're waiting for something bad to happen?\n\nPatient: Waiting for something bad to happen? Um... no, not really. I mean, I don't sit around expecting the sky to fall or anything. I just go about my day, you know?\n\nThough, I will say, waiting for things in general is getting ridiculous lately. Have you tried calling customer service for anything recently? I had to call my internet provider on Tuesday because the Wi-Fi at my apartment kept dropping. Like, every twenty minutes, it would just cut out. So I call them up, and I swear I was on hold for an hour and forty-five minutes. And they play that awful, repetitive elevator music that loops every thirty seconds. It gets stuck in your head for the rest of the day. \n\nWhen I finally got a real person, it was this guy who clearly had no idea what he was doing. He just kept reading from a script, telling me to unplug the router and plug it back in. Like, obviously I already tried that! I'm not an idiot. Then he tried to tell me I needed to upgrade to their premium package for an extra thirty bucks a month if I wanted a \"more stable connection.\" It's such a scam. They throttle your speed on purpose just so they can upsell you. My neighbor switched to that fiber optic company that just moved into town, and he says it's amazing, but they have to drill a hole in your wall to install it, and my landlord is super strict about that kind of stuff. He threw a fit last year just because I used pushpins to hang up a calendar. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? Oh, right, waiting for bad stuff. No, I don't really do that. I'm not waiting for disaster or anything. I just hate waiting on hold.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Detached? Like, from my body or something? Uh, no. I don't think I've ever felt like that. I'm pretty much just right here, you know? Paying attention to my surroundings. \n\nActually, speaking of surroundings, the scenery over by the new development on the east side is completely unrecognizable now. Have you been over there recently? They tore down that entire strip of woods where the old drive-in theater used to be. It's just completely gone. Now it's all these cookie-cutter townhouses that look exactly the same. They're painted these weird shades of gray and beige. I had a delivery there on Tuesday, and I swear I drove around in circles for like fifteen minutes because none of the streets have signs up yet. And the GPS hasn't updated, so my phone just keeps telling me I'm driving through an empty field. \n\nIt drains my battery like crazy when the GPS gets confused like that. I actually had to buy one of those portable power banks, but I couldn't even use it yesterday because my cat knocked the charging cable off the counter and hid it. He does that with everything. Last week he batted my van keys under the fridge and I was late for my shift. My supervisor, Greg, gave me this whole lecture about punctuality, which is incredibly rich coming from a guy who takes two-hour lunch breaks to go to the driving range. I tried to point that out, but he just threatened to put me on the graveyard shift. \n\nBut, wait, what was the question? Detached? Yeah, no, I don't feel detached from myself or anything. I'm just trying to figure out where I'm going half the time without my phone dying.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Trouble sleeping? Yeah, actually. I barely sleep now. I'll maybe doze off for an hour or two, but then I'm up from some dream about that cell, or I'll hear a door slamming and just jolt awake. And by morning, I feel way too wrecked to drive my route. \n\nWhich is a huge problem right now because they just completely changed the delivery zones for the downtown area, and it is a total disaster. My supervisor, Greg—the one I was telling you about—decided we needed to \"optimize\" our routes using this new software the corporate office bought. But the software doesn't account for one-way streets or the construction on 5th Avenue. So yesterday, it routed me right into this narrow alley that was completely blocked off by a garbage truck. Those sanitation guys take forever, too. I was sitting there for twenty minutes watching this guy slowly empty dumpsters while he was casually talking on his phone. And you can't even back out of that alley because people park right up against the curb. I had to just sit there listening to the radio, and every single station was playing commercials. Have you noticed how many commercials there are for sports betting apps now? It's like every other ad is some actor telling you to bet on football. It's getting out of hand. My buddy Dave lost like three hundred bucks last weekend betting on a game because he thought he had a \"sure thing.\" I told him the house always wins, but he never listens to me. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what was the question? Sleep? Right, yeah. I barely sleep. Just waking up from those dreams and feeling too wrecked to drive.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you are on the lookout for potential threats or dangers in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Threats or dangers? No, not really. I mean, I'm not walking around looking over my shoulder or expecting someone to jump out at me. I'm just trying to get through the day, you know? \n\nSpeaking of getting through the day, though, you wouldn't believe the hazard it is just trying to get a decent cup of coffee around here anymore. That little diner I usually stop at on 3rd Street? They completely changed their menu, and now everything is, like, artisanal and overpriced. I just wanted a regular black coffee, and the barista—who was wearing this ridiculous leather apron, by the way—started asking me if I wanted a pour-over or a cold brew or some single-origin roast from Guatemala. Like, buddy, I'm in a delivery van, I just need caffeine. And then he charged me six dollars! Six dollars for a plain coffee! \n\nAnd the cups they use now are those compostable ones, which sounds great for the environment, I guess, but the lids don't stay on. I hit one pothole on Elm Street—and the potholes on Elm are basically craters right now because the city hasn't fixed them since winter—and the lid pops off, splashing hot coffee all over my dashboard. I had to use half a roll of paper towels to clean it up, and it still smells like burnt beans in my van. Plus, it got all over the manifest clipboard. Greg, my supervisor, is going to throw an absolute fit if he sees coffee stains on the paperwork. He treats those manifests like they're sacred documents or something, even though everything is supposed to be digital now anyway. \n\nBut, uh... what were we talking about? Oh, right, looking out for danger. No, I don't really do that. I'm not on the lookout for threats or anything. Just trying to avoid potholes and overpriced coffee.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're constantly watching for something to go wrong?\n\nPatient: Watching for something to go wrong? I'm already wearing a thermal shirt and a hoodie under my uniform jacket. I look like a marshmallow. If I broke my foot, I don't even know how workers' comp would handle it, because Greg still hasn't filed the paperwork from when Dave twisted his ankle on the loading ramp last month. Dave's been limping around the depot complaining about it to anyone who will listen. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Watching for something to go wrong? Oh, right. No, I don't really do that. Left his office door wide open, computer still on, half-eaten sandwich on his desk. Tuna salad, I think, because the whole hallway smelled like it. Which is so hypocritical, by the way, because last week he sent out this mass email to all the drivers saying we weren't allowed to eat strong-smelling foods in the breakroom anymore. Someone microwaved leftover salmon, and Greg threw an absolute fit. Who even talks like that? \n\nAnyway, so Greg is just gone, right? And I had this package that needed a supervisor's override because the barcode got torn off. I got a ticket last Thursday for being two minutes over my meter. Two minutes! I was literally walking out of the lobby of the office building. I buy the good milk, too—the lactose-free kind, because regular milk upsets my stomach. It's like five dollars a carton. \n\nAnd don't even get me started on the laundry situation in my building. People just leave their wet clothes sitting in the other two machines for hours. I actually had to take someone's damp towels out and put them on top of the dryer on Sunday because I'd been waiting for an hour and a half to wash my uniforms. If I don't wash my uniforms, Greg writes me up. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Avoiding talking about it? Yeah, I guess I do. I just... They throttle your speed on purpose just so they can upsell you. My neighbor switched to that fiber optic company that just moved into town, and he says it's amazing, but they have to drill a hole in your wall to install it, and my landlord is super strict about that kind of stuff. He threw a fit last year just because I used pushpins to hang up a calendar. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? Oh, right, waiting for bad stuff. No, I don't really do that. I'm not waiting for disaster or anything. I just hate waiting on hold.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Detached? Like, from my body or something? Uh, no. I don't think I've ever felt like that. I'm pretty much just right here, you know? Paying attention to my surroundings. \n\nActually, speaking of surroundings, the scenery over by the new development on the east side is completely unrecognizable now. Have you been over there recently? They tore down that entire strip of woods where the old drive-in theater used to be. It's just completely gone. Now it's all these cookie-cutter townhouses that look exactly the same. They're painted these weird shades of gray and beige. I had a delivery there on Tuesday, and I swear I drove around in circles for like fifteen minutes because none of the streets have signs up yet. And the GPS hasn't updated, so my phone just keeps telling me I'm driving through an empty field. \n\nIt drains my battery like crazy when the GPS gets confused like that. I actually had to buy one of those portable power banks, but I couldn't even use it yesterday because my cat knocked the charging cable off the counter and hid it. He does that with everything. Last week he batted my van keys under the fridge and I was late for my shift. My supervisor, Greg, gave me this whole lecture about punctuality, which is incredibly rich coming from a guy who takes two-hour lunch breaks to go to the driving range. I tried to point that out, but he just threatened to put me on the graveyard shift. \n\nBut, wait, what was the question? Detached? Yeah, no, I don't feel detached from myself or anything. I'm just trying to figure out where I'm going half the time without my phone dying.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Trouble sleeping? Yeah, actually. I barely sleep now. I'll maybe doze off for an hour or two, but then I'm up from some dream about that cell, or I'll hear a door slamming and just jolt awake. And by morning, I feel way too wrecked to drive my route. \n\nWhich is a huge problem right now because they just completely changed the delivery zones for the downtown area, and it is a total disaster. My supervisor, Greg—the one I was telling you about—decided we needed to \"optimize\" our routes using this new software the corporate office bought. But the software doesn't account for one-way streets or the construction on 5th Avenue. So yesterday, it routed me right into this narrow alley that was completely blocked off by a garbage truck. Those sanitation guys take forever, too. I was sitting there for twenty minutes watching this guy slowly empty dumpsters while he was casually talking on his phone. And you can't even back out of that alley because people park right up against the curb. I had to just sit there listening to the radio, and every single station was playing commercials. Have you noticed how many commercials there are for sports betting apps now? It's like every other ad is some actor telling you to bet on football. It's getting out of hand. My buddy Dave lost like three hundred bucks last weekend betting on a game because he thought he had a \"sure thing.\" I told him the house always wins, but he never listens to me. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what was the question? Sleep? Right, yeah. I barely sleep. Just waking up from those dreams and feeling too wrecked to drive.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you are on the lookout for potential threats or dangers in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Threats or dangers? No, not really. I mean, I'm not walking around looking over my shoulder or expecting someone to jump out at me. I'm just trying to get through the day, you know? \n\nSpeaking of getting through the day, though, you wouldn't believe the hazard it is just trying to get a decent cup of coffee around here anymore. That little diner I usually stop at on 3rd Street? They completely changed their menu, and now everything is, like, artisanal and overpriced. I just wanted a regular black coffee, and the barista—who was wearing this ridiculous leather apron, by the way—started asking me if I wanted a pour-over or a cold brew or some single-origin roast from Guatemala. Like, buddy, I'm in a delivery van, I just need caffeine. And then he charged me six dollars! Six dollars for a plain coffee! \n\nAnd the cups they use now are those compostable ones, which sounds great for the environment, I guess, but the lids don't stay on. I hit one pothole on Elm Street—and the potholes on Elm are basically craters right now because the city hasn't fixed them since winter—and the lid pops off, splashing hot coffee all over my dashboard. I had to use half a roll of paper towels to clean it up, and it still smells like burnt beans in my van. Plus, it got all over the manifest clipboard. Greg, my supervisor, is going to throw an absolute fit if he sees coffee stains on the paperwork. He treats those manifests like they're sacred documents or something, even though everything is supposed to be digital now anyway. \n\nBut, uh... what were we talking about? Oh, right, looking out for danger. No, I don't really do that. I'm not on the lookout for threats or anything. Just trying to avoid potholes and overpriced coffee.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're constantly watching for something to go wrong?\n\nPatient: Watching for something to go wrong? I mean, no, not really. I'm not, like, anticipating disaster at every turn or anything. I just kind of do my job. \n\nThough, honestly, speaking of things going wrong, you should see the new sorting machine they installed at the depot. It is an absolute nightmare. They spent I don't even know how many thousands of dollars on this automated conveyor system that's supposed to scan barcodes and route packages to the right loading bays. But the scanner gets jammed if a label is even slightly wrinkled. And since it's an automated system, when one box jams, the whole line stops. Every single morning this week, we've had to stand around for like forty-five minutes while some tech guy in a high-vis vest climbs up there with a flashlight to unjam it. \n\nAnd the worst part is, while we're standing there, the warehouse gets freezing cold. They keep the loading dock doors open, and the heating system in that building is basically nonexistent. I brought it up to Greg—my supervisor—and he told me to \"layer up.\" Layer up! I'm already wearing a thermal shirt and a hoodie under my uniform jacket. I look like a marshmallow. Plus, you can't even move properly to carry the heavier boxes when you're wearing that much clothing. I nearly dropped a mini-fridge on my foot on Wednesday because my sleeves were so bulky I couldn't get a good grip on the straps. If I broke my foot, I don't even know how workers' comp would handle it, because Greg still hasn't filed the paperwork from when Dave twisted his ankle on the loading ramp last month. Dave's been limping around the depot complaining about it to anyone who will listen. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Watching for something to go wrong? Oh, right. No, I don't really do that. I just deal with things when they happen, you know? Like the sorting machine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible for what happened in that holding room?\n\nPatient: Guilty? No, why would I feel guilty? I didn't do anything wrong in there. I didn't cause it. I was just... there. \n\nSpeaking of being there, though, you know who wasn't there when I got back to the depot yesterday? Greg. He just vanished around 2:00 PM. Left his office door wide open, computer still on, half-eaten sandwich on his desk. Tuna salad, I think, because the whole hallway smelled like it. Which is so hypocritical, by the way, because last week he sent out this mass email to all the drivers saying we weren't allowed to eat strong-smelling foods in the breakroom anymore. Someone microwaved leftover salmon, and Greg threw an absolute fit. He printed out these laminated signs and taped them to the microwaves. \"Be considerate of your coworkers' olfactory space.\" Olfactory space! Who even talks like that? \n\nAnyway, so Greg is just gone, right? And I had this package that needed a supervisor's override because the barcode got torn off. The customer was this older lady who called the depot three times asking where her grandson's birthday present was. So I'm standing there with this box, waiting for Greg, and finally, Dave tells me he saw Greg get into his car and drive off. Apparently, he had some \"emergency meeting\" at the corporate office. But Dave thinks he just left early to go play golf, because Dave saw his clubs in the backseat of his car on Tuesday. Dave is always snooping around people's cars in the lot. He says he's just \"observant,\" but honestly, it's a little weird. Like, why are you looking into the back seat of Greg's Honda Civic, Dave? Just load your van. \n\nBut, uh... what were we talking about? Guilt? Right, no. I don't feel responsible for what happened. I didn't have anything to do with it. Just wrong place, wrong time, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious for no apparent reason?\n\nPatient: Anxious for no reason? No, I don't think so. If I'm stressed out, there's always a reason for it. I don't just sit around feeling anxious out of nowhere. \n\nBut man, if you want a reason to be stressed, you should try finding a commercial loading zone on 5th Avenue before noon. It is literally impossible. And the city just hired all these new parking enforcement officers, and they are completely ruthless. They don't even use the chalk on the tires anymore to mark how long you've been there. They have these little cameras mounted on their carts that scan your license plate as they drive by. I got a ticket last Thursday for being two minutes over my meter. Two minutes! I was literally walking out of the lobby of the office building. I waved at the guy, like, hey, I'm right here, I'm leaving! And he just printed the little slip and stuck it under my wiper without even making eye contact with me. Sixty-five bucks! \n\nAnd the worst part is, the company doesn't reimburse us for parking tickets unless we can prove the loading zone was illegally blocked by a civilian vehicle. Which, by the way, happens all the time. People just throw their hazard lights on and think it's a magic park-anywhere button. Just yesterday, I saw this guy in a silver BMW parked right in the middle of the yellow curb outside the bank, eating a bagel. Just sitting there! I had to park a block away and haul three boxes of printer paper down the sidewalk. I honked at him when I drove past later, and he actually flipped me off! Can you believe that? \n\nAnyway, uh... what did you ask me? Anxious for no reason? Yeah, no, I don't get that. I only get anxious when there's an actual reason. Like the parking guys.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid thinking about or talking about what happened in that holding room?\n\nPatient: Avoid thinking about it? I mean, I guess I have been taking the long way around town lately. I can't even make myself go near that detention building anymore. I've even been turning down night runs just to make sure I don't get routed near there. If I even try to talk about it or think about what happened, it feels like I'm right back in that room.\n\nWhich, by the way, turning down night runs is really messing with my paycheck. The night premium is an extra two dollars an hour, and that adds up, you know? Especially right now, because my landlord just raised my rent. He sent this letter saying it's due to \"increased property taxes and maintenance costs,\" but he hasn't fixed a single thing in that apartment since I moved in. The gasket on the refrigerator door has been peeling off for six months. I have to push a kitchen chair against it to keep it shut tight, otherwise the cold air leaks out and all my milk goes bad. I buy the good milk, too—the lactose-free kind, because regular milk upsets my stomach. It's like five dollars a carton. \n\nAnd don't even get me started on the laundry situation in my building. There are three washers for twenty units, and one of them has had an \"Out of Order\" sign taped to it since Thanksgiving. Thanksgiving! It's ridiculous. People just leave their wet clothes sitting in the other two machines for hours. I actually had to take someone's damp towels out and put them on top of the dryer on Sunday because I'd been waiting for an hour and a half to wash my uniforms. If I don't wash my uniforms, Greg writes me up. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Avoiding talking about it? Yeah, I guess I do. I just... I can't go near that building or talk about it without feeling like I'm right back there. It's too much.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Interfered with work? Yeah, definitely. I've already missed two shifts this week. And even when I try to go in, I just... I don't feel safe driving the van or handling deliveries. I'm just too shaken up, constantly on edge, you know? 

Speaking of driving the van, though, they just gave me one of the new electric ones on Monday. Have you seen those? They look like giant, rolling marshmallows. They're so quiet, which you'd think is a good thing, but it's actually a nightmare because pedestrians don't hear you coming. I was pulling into an alley off 8th Street, and this guy was just standing right in the middle of the driveway, looking at his phone, completely oblivious. I couldn't honk because the horn on those new vans sounds like a toy train, it's embarrassing. And the regenerative braking on those things? It's so aggressive. You take your foot off the accelerator and it throws you forward against the seatbelt. My neck has been killing me since I started driving it. I asked Greg if I could have my old gas van back, the Ford, but he said corporate is trying to reduce our carbon footprint. Like my one van is gonna save the ice caps while there's a literal factory down the river pumping out black smoke all day.

And it's not just work, either. I had to stop showing up for my volunteer commitments, too. I help out at the animal shelter on weekends, walking the big dogs, but I just... I can't keep it together around people right now. 

Which sucks, because there's this one dog, Buster, he's a pit mix, and he only likes me. He's got this weird quirk where he won't walk on grass if it's wet. He'll literally tip-toe around the puddles on the sidewalk. It's hilarious. But the other volunteers don't have the patience for him, they just try to drag him along, and he digs his heels in. I brought him a squeaky hamburger toy last month, and he destroyed it in about four seconds. Just ripped the squeaker right out. 

Anyway, uh, what was the question? Oh, interfering with stuff. Yeah, I've missed shifts, and I can't do my volunteer stuff. I'm just too on edge to function normally right now.
